I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a sequence of poker hands with results: W (win), L (loss), D (draw), and ? (unknown)
2. We need to replace all ? with W, L, or D to create a valid sequence
3. Valid sequence conditions:
   - Final absolute difference between wins and losses = k
   - At no point during the sequence does the absolute difference between wins and losses reach k
4. We need to output any valid sequence, or "NO" if impossible

## Key Insights

1. Draws (D) don't affect the win/loss difference
2. We need to track the running difference between wins and losses
3. We cannot have the absolute difference equal to k at any point (including the end)
4. The final difference must be exactly k

## Approach

1. First, I'll count the current W, L, and D characters
2. The final difference must be k, so if we have w wins and l losses, |w - l| = k
3. We need to be careful about intermediate steps - we can never reach exactly k difference
4. Strategy: try to build the sequence from left to right, making optimal choices for ? characters
5. For each ?, we have three choices: W, L, or D

## Critical Constraint

The key constraint is that we must never reach exactly k difference at any point. This means:
- If the current difference is d, we can't make a move that makes it exactly k
- We can only make moves that keep us strictly away from k

## Algorithm

1. Simulate the sequence from left to right
2. For each position, try to choose a character that doesn't violate the constraints
3. Use a greedy approach: try to keep the difference as close to 0 as possible to avoid hitting k
4. For the final result, ensure the absolute difference is exactly k

Let me implement this:

```python
def solve():
    n, k = map(int, input().split())
    s = input().strip()
    
    # Convert string to list for easier manipulation
    result = list(s)
    
    # Count current wins and losses
    wins = 0
    losses = 0
    total_diff = 0
    
    # First pass: try to determine if a valid sequence exists
    # We'll simulate the process and see if we can avoid reaching difference k
    
    def is_valid(sequence):
        diff = 0
        for char in sequence:
            if char == 'W':
                diff += 1
            elif char == 'L':
                diff -= 1
            # D doesn't change diff
            if abs(diff) == k:
                return False
        return abs(diff) == k
    
    # Use a greedy approach: for each '?', try to choose a value that doesn't cause us to hit k
    # and see if we can achieve total difference of k at the end
    
    # First, let's try to be as conservative as possible
    # We'll try to avoid increasing the difference too much
    
    # Try all possible assignments using backtracking might be too expensive
    # Instead, let's use a greedy approach with constraints
    
    # Count what we already have
    current_wins = 0
    current_losses = 0
    current_diff = 0
    
    # We need to be careful about the final difference being exactly k
    # and never hitting k in between
    
    # Let's try a constructive approach
    result = []
    for char in s:
        if char == 'W':
            result.append('W')
            current_wins += 1
            current_diff += 1
        elif char == 'L':
            result.append('L')
            current_losses += 1
            current_diff -= 1
        elif char == 'D':
            result.append('D')
            # diff remains same
        else:  # char == '?'
            # Try to choose a character that doesn't make diff = k
            # and that helps us reach total diff of k at the end
            chosen = None
            
            # Try D first (safest)
            if abs(current_diff) != k:
                chosen = 'D'
            else:
                # If current diff is k, we can't choose anything that doesn't maintain this, 
                # but if current_diff is k, this would be invalid, so we need to avoid this
                # So we need to make a choice that won't make diff = k
                # Try W and L, whichever doesn't make diff = k
                if current_diff + 1 != k and current_diff + 1 != -k:
                    chosen = 'W'
                elif current_diff - 1 != k and current_diff - 1 != -k:
                    chosen = 'L'
                else:
                    # Both W and L would make diff = k, which is invalid
                    # This means our previous state already violated the constraint
                    chosen = None
            
            if chosen is not None:
                result.append(chosen)
                if chosen == 'W':
                    current_wins += 1
                    current_diff += 1
                elif chosen == 'L':
                    current_losses += 1
                    current_diff -= 1
                # D doesn't change anything
            else:
                # This means we have no valid choice at this position
                return "NO"
    
    # Check if the final difference is exactly k
    if abs(current_diff) != k:
        return "NO"
    
    # Also check that we never hit k difference during the process
    # We already checked at each step in our simulation
    
    # But we need to verify at each step
    max_diff = 0
    diff = 0
    valid = True
    for char in result:
        if char == 'W':
            diff += 1
        elif char == 'L':
            diff -= 1
        # D does nothing
        if abs(diff) == k:
            valid = False
            break
    
    if valid and abs(diff) == k:
        return ''.join(result)
    else:
        return "NO"

# Actually, the above greedy approach might not work because we need to ensure we can reach exactly k at the end
# Let's think differently - we need to take into account that we can manipulate final difference

def solve():
    n, k = map(int, input().split())
    s = input().strip()
    
    # We'll try a different approach
    # The key insight: we can determine what the final difference must be
    # and work backwards from there
    
    # But let's first check if it's possible at all
    
    # Count fixed W, L, D
    fixed_w = s.count('W')
    fixed_l = s.count('L')
    fixed_d = s.count('D')
    
    # We need to assign values to ? such that:
    # final |w - l| = k
    # and |diff at any step| < k (not equal to k)
    
    # Let's try a greedy approach with constraints
    
    # We'll simulate the entire sequence
    result = []
    wins = 0
    losses = 0
    current_diff = 0
    
    for i, char in enumerate(s):
        if char == 'W':
            result.append('W')
            wins += 1
            current_diff += 1
        elif char == 'L':
            result.append('L')
            losses += 1
            current_diff -= 1
        elif char == 'D':
            result.append('D')
            # nothing changes
        else:  # char == '?'
            # Try to find a choice that doesn't make current_diff = k
            # and that can help us reach final difference k
            
            # First, check if we can choose D
            if abs(current_diff) != k:
                result.append('D')
                # no change
            else:
                # We cannot choose D because it would keep current_diff = k
                # So we need to choose W or L
                # But choosing either would make |diff| = k or something else
                
                # Try W first
                new_diff = current_diff + 1
                if abs(new_diff) != k:
                    result.append('W')
                    current_diff += 1
                elif abs(current_diff - 1) != k:  # try L
                    result.append('L')
                    current_diff -= 1
                else:
                    # Both choices would make |diff| = k
                    return "NO"
    
    # Now verify the final difference
    final_diff = abs(wins - losses)
    if final_diff != k:
        return "NO"
    
    # Verify no intermediate difference was k
    diff = 0
    valid = True
    for c in result:
        if c == 'W':
            diff += 1
        elif c == 'L':
            diff -= 1
        if abs(diff) == k:
            valid = False
            break
    
    if valid:
        return ''.join(result)
    else:
        return "NO"

print(solve())
```

### Answer: (use the provided format with backticks)

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a sequence of poker results with characters W (win), L (loss), D (draw), and ? (unknown)
2. We need to replace all ? with W, L, or D to satisfy two conditions:
   - Final absolute difference between wins and losses = k
   - At no point during the sequence should the absolute difference reach k (before the final step)

3. A draw (D) doesn't change the difference between wins and losses.

## Key Insights

1. The absolute difference between wins and losses starts at 0 and changes by +1 for W, -1 for L, and 0 for D.
2. We need to ensure that we never reach exactly k or -k during the game (except at the very end).
3. At the end, the absolute difference must be exactly k.

## Approach

1. First, let's count the current wins, losses, and draws from the known characters.
2. For each position, we need to decide what to put in the ? positions.
3. We need to maintain a running difference (wins - losses) and ensure it never reaches ±k before the end.
4. At the end, we need the absolute difference to be exactly k.

Let me think about this more systematically:

- Let `diff` be the current difference (wins - losses)
- We start at diff = 0
- After each hand, diff changes by: +1 for W, -1 for L, 0 for D
- We must never have |diff| = k before the last hand
- After the last hand, we must have |diff| = k

## Strategy

I'll use a greedy approach with backtracking or simulation:

1. Count fixed W, L, D in the string
2. Simulate the process, deciding what to put in ? positions
3. For each ?, try to place values that keep us safe from hitting k or -k
4. At the end, check if we reach exactly k

Since we can only make local decisions, let me think of a simpler approach:

**Key observation**: We can control the final difference by deciding how many W and L to place. The final difference = (number of W - number of L) = k or -k.

But we must avoid reaching exactly k or -k at any intermediate step.

Let me implement a simulation-based solution:

```python
def solve():
    n, k = map(int, input().split())
    s = input().strip()
    
    # Convert string to list for mutability
    seq = list(s)
    
    # Count current W, L, D
    current_wins = 0
    current_losses = 0
    current_draws = 0
    
    for c in s:
        if c == 'W':
            current_wins += 1
        elif c == 'L':
            current_losses += 1
        elif c == 'D':
            current_draws += 1
    
    # Try to construct a valid sequence
    # We need final |w - l| = k
    # and |w - l| never equals k during the game (except at the end)
    
    # Try to assign values to ? positions
    # We can be smart about it: we want to avoid hitting k or -k
    
    def is_valid_assignment(assignment):
        diff = 0
        for i in range(n):
            if assignment[i] == 'W':
                diff += 1
            elif assignment[i] == 'L':
                diff -= 1
            # D doesn't change diff
            if abs(diff) == k:
                return False  # Hit k at some point
        return abs(diff) == k
    
    # Try to build assignment greedily
    # We need to decide what goes into each ?
    
    # Let's first try to assign all ? to D (neutral)
    # Then see if we can adjust to reach final difference k
    
    # Count how many ? we have
    q_count = seq.count('?')
    
    # Start with all ? as D
    result = []
    w_count = current_wins
    l_count = current_losses
    d_count = current_draws
    
    for c in seq:
        if c == '?':
            result.append('D')
        else:
            result.append(c)
    
    # Now we have a sequence with all ? as D
    # Check if it's valid
    diff = 0
    valid = True
    for i in range(n):
        if result[i] == 'W':
            diff += 1
        elif result[i] == 'L':
            diff -= 1
        if abs(diff) == k:
            valid = False
            break
    
    if valid and abs(diff) == k:
        return ''.join(result)
    
    # If not valid, we need to convert some ? to W or L
    # We need to change some D to W or L to make final difference k
    # But we must avoid hitting k at any point
    
    # Try all possible combinations of changing some ? to W or L
    # This might be exponential, so need a smarter approach
    
    # Better approach: greedy with constraints
    # Let's try to make the final difference k by adjusting the counts
    
    # We need w - l = k or w - l = -k
    # So w - l = d, where d = ±k
    
    # We can only change ? positions
    
    # For each ? position, we can choose W, L, or D
    
    # Let's try to solve this with a greedy simulation
    # We'll build the sequence step by step
    
    # First, let's try to make w - l = k
    # Then try w - l = -k
    
    def build_sequence(target_difference):
        # target_difference is the final difference (w - l)
        # We need to assign ? to achieve this difference
        # without hitting |diff| == k at any intermediate step
        
        # Count current w, l, and available ?
        w_count = current_wins
        l_count = current_losses
        q_count = seq.count('?')
        
        # We need final w - l = target_difference
        # So we need to add w_new - l_new = target_difference - (w_count - l_count)
        # where w_new + l_new = q_count (if we assign all ? to W/L), or less if some are D
        
        # Actually, if we assign some ? to D, then w_new + l_new < q_count
        
        # Let's try to assign all ? to either W or L
        
        # We need to find a subset of ? to assign to W and the rest to D or L
        # such that the final difference is target_difference
        
        # This is complex, let me try a different approach
        
        # Try to assign in a way that keeps diff away from k
        
        # Start with all ? as D
        seq_copy = seq[:]
        diff = 0
        
        # We'll try to convert some ? to W or L
        # We need to reach final difference = target_difference
        # and never hit |diff| == k
        
        # First, calculate what extra difference we need
        current_diff = w_count - l_count
        needed_extra = target_difference - current_diff
        
        # We need to add extra = needed_extra from the ? positions
        # We can convert a ? to W (+1) or L (-1)
        
        # Try to convert as few ? as possible
        # We need to find a combination of converting some ? to W or L such that sum of changes = needed_extra
        
        # And at no point during the process should |diff| == k
        
        # This is still complex - let me try a simpler greedy
        
        # Greedy: whenever we have a choice, choose a value that keeps us safe
        # and helps us reach the target
        
        # Build sequence with all ? as D initially
        result = seq[:]
        diff = 0
        valid = True
        
        # We'll go through each position and decide what to put
        # For each ?, we try to put a value that doesn't make |diff| == k
        # and helps us reach target
        
        for i in range(n):
            if seq[i] == '?':
                # Try each option
                # First, try to keep it as D (safe)
                # Then, if needed, convert to W or L
                options = []
                if diff + 1 < k and diff + 1 >= -k:  # W would make diff +1
                    options.append('W')
                if diff - 1 > -k and diff - 1 <= k:  # L would make diff -1  
                    options.append('L')
                options.append('D')  # Always safe
                
                # Choose the option that helps us get closer to target
                # But we also need to consider future possibilities
                
                # For simplicity, let's try to keep it as D first
                # Then later adjust
                
                if 'D' in options:
                    result[i] = 'D'
                else:
                    # If all options would violate safety, this is invalid
                    valid = False
                    break
            else:
                if seq[i] == 'W':
                    diff += 1
                elif seq[i] == 'L':
                    diff -= 1
        
        # Check if we hit k at any point
        diff = 0
        for i in range(n):
            if result[i] == 'W':
                diff += 1
            elif result[i] == 'L':
                diff -= 1
            if abs(diff) == k:
                valid = False
                break
        
        if valid and abs(diff) == abs(target_difference):
            return ''.join(result)
        
        return None
    
    # Try target_difference = k first
    result1 = build_sequence(k)
    if result1 is not None:
        return result1
    
    # Try target_difference = -k
    result2 = build_sequence(-k)
    if result2 is not None:
        return result2
    
    return "NO"

# Actually, my approach is getting too complex. Let me implement a cleaner solution.

def solve():
    n, k = map(int, input().split())
    s = input().strip()
    
    # We need to find an assignment to ? such that:
    # 1. |final_wins - final_losses| = k
    # 2. At no point during the sequence does |difference| = k (except at the end)
    
    # Count current W, L, D
    w_count = s.count('W')
    l_count = s.count('L')
    d_count = s.count('D')
    q_count = s.count('?')
    
    # Final difference must be ±k
    # Let's try to achieve final difference = k first
    
    # We can assign the ? to W, L, or D
    # Let x be number of ? assigned to W
    # Let y be number of ? assigned to L
    # Then x + y <= q_count (we can put some ? as D)
    # Final difference = (w_count + x) - (l_count + y) = (w_count - l_count) + (x - y)
    # We want this to equal k or -k
    
    # So we need (w_count - l_count) + (x - y) = ±k
    
    # For final difference = k: x - y = k - (w_count - l_count)
    # For final difference = -k: x - y = -k - (w_count - l_count)
    
    # Let's try to solve for final difference = k first
    target_diff1 = k
    needed_diff1 = target_diff1 - (w_count - l_count)
    
    # Can we achieve needed_diff1 with x, y >= 0, x + y <= q_count?
    # x - y = needed_diff1
    # x + y = some value between 0 and q_count
    
    # Solving: x = (needed_diff1 + s) / 2, y = (s - needed_diff1) / 2
    # where s = x + y
    
    # We need s >= |needed_diff1| and s <= q_count
    # and both x, y are non-negative integers
    
    # Try all possible s from max(0, |needed_diff1|) to q_count
    found1 = False
    assignment1 = None
    
    for s in range(max(0, abs(needed_diff1)), q_count + 1):
        if (needed_diff1 + s) % 2 == 0 and (s - needed_diff1) % 2 == 0:
            x = (needed_diff1 + s) // 2
            y = (s - needed_diff1) // 2
            if x >= 0 and y >= 0 and x + y <= q_count:
                # We can achieve this
                # Now we need to assign the values to the ? positions such that
                # we never reach |diff| = k during the process
                
                # Let's try to assign in a way that keeps us safe
                result = list(s)
                diff = 0
                
                # We'll use a greedy approach: assign D first, then convert some to W/L
                # We need to assign exactly x W's and y L's among the ? positions
                # we can choose which positions to assign
                
                # To avoid hitting k, we can assign W/L in a way that doesn't cause immediate jump
                
                # First, create base with all ? as D
                temp_result = list(s)
                q_positions = []
                for i, c in enumerate(s):
                    if c == '?':
                        q_positions.append(i)
                
                # We need to assign x of these to W and y to L
                # Let's assign them in a way that keeps diff away from k
                
                # We'll try to minimize the risk of hitting k
                # For each ? position, if assigning W or L would cause |diff| to become k, we skip
                
                # Actually, let's assign in order and simulate
                
                # We'll assign the assignments to positions in order
                # We'll use a greedy choice: when we have a choice, avoid making |diff| = k
                
                pos_idx = 0
                w_assigned = 0
                l_assigned = 0
                result = list(s)
                
                # First assign all ? to D
                for i, c in enumerate(s):
                    if c == '?':
                        result[i] = 'D'
                
                # Now assign x W's and y L's
                # We'll try to find a valid sequence
                
                # Simple greedy: assign W when it helps us reach target without hitting k
                # But we have to ensure we don't hit k at any point
                
                # Let's actually just try to assign and check the whole sequence
                # We need to choose which x positions to make W and which y to make L
                
                # For simplicity, let's assign in a way that at each step, |diff| < k
                # We can assign W or L only if it keeps us safe
                
                # Since we need to do this for all possible assignments, let's try a different approach
                
                # Try to build the sequence greedily for this specific assignment
                
                # We have x W's and y L's to place among q_positions
                # We can choose any x positions for W and any y positions for L (with x+y <= q_count)
                
                # Let's assign in order, and choose the option that keeps us safe and helps us reach target
                
                # Actually, since we have flexibility, we can just check if there exists ANY assignment that works
                
                # Simple: assign W to the first x positions and L to the next y positions
                temp_result = list(s)
                q_idx = 0
                w_idx = 0
                l_idx = 0
                
                for i, c in enumerate(s):
                    if c == '?':
                        if w_idx < x and q_idx < len(q_positions) and w_idx + l_idx < x + y:
                            # Check if assigning W would make |diff| == k
                            diff_current = diff
                            # We need to calculate diff before this position
                            # But we don't have the full history
                    
                    # This is getting too complex. Let me restart with a simpler approach.
                
                # For the purpose of this problem, if we can't find a valid assignment, return NO
                
                # Let's just try to build a simple assignment and check if it works
                # We'll assign the first x ? to W, the next y to L, the rest to D
                
                temp_result = list(s)
                q_positions = [i for i, c in enumerate(s) if c == '?']
                
                # Assign first x to W, next y to L
                if len(q_positions) < x + y:
                    continue
                    
                w_idx = 0
                l_idx = 0
                for i, c in enumerate(s):
                    if c == '?':
                        if w_idx < x:
                            temp_result[i] = 'W'
                            w_idx += 1
                        elif l_idx < y:
                            temp_result[i] = 'L'
                            l_idx += 1
                        else:
                            temp_result[i] = 'D'
                
                # Now check if this assignment works
                diff = 0
                valid = True
                for i in range(n):
                    if temp_result[i] == 'W':
                        diff += 1
                    elif temp_result[i] == 'L':
                        diff -= 1
                    if abs(diff) == k:
                        valid = False
                        break
                
                if valid and abs(diff) == k:
                    return ''.join(temp_result)
        
        # Try final difference = -k
        target_diff2 = -k
        needed_diff2 = target_diff2 - (w_count - l_count)
        
        for s in range(max(0, abs(needed_diff2)), q_count + 1):
            if (needed_diff2 + s) % 2 == 0 and (s - needed_diff2) % 2 == 0:
                x = (needed_diff2 + s) // 2
                y = (s - needed_diff2) // 2
                if x >= 0 and y >= 0 and x + y <= q_count:
                    # Try to build assignment
                    temp_result = list(s)
                    q_positions = [i for i, c in enumerate(s) if c == '?']
                    
                    if len(q_positions) < x + y:
                        continue
                    
                    w_idx = 0
                    l_idx = 0
                    for i, c in enumerate(s):
                        if c == '?':
                            if w_idx < x:
                                temp_result[i] = 'W'
                                w_idx += 1
                            elif l_idx < y:
                                temp_result[i] = 'L'
                                l_idx += 1
                            else:
                                temp_result[i] = 'D'
                    
                    diff = 0
                    valid = True
                    for i in range(n):
                        if temp_result[i] == 'W':
                            diff += 1
                        elif temp_result[i] == 'L':
                            diff -= 1
                        if abs(diff) == k:
                            valid = False
                            break
                    
                    if valid and abs(diff) == k:
                        return ''.join(temp_result)
        
        return "NO"
    
    return "NO"

# After multiple attempts, I realize this is quite complex. Let me implement a simpler and more reliable solution.

def solve():
    n, k = map(int, input().split())
    s = input().strip()
    
    # Try all possible assignments of ? to W, L, D
    # Since n, k <= 1000, but number of ? can be up to 1000, we can't try all combinations
    
    # Instead, use the following insight:
    # We need to avoid |diff| = k at any intermediate step
    # We can control the final difference by choosing how many W and L to add
    
    # First, calculate the current difference
    current_diff = 0
    w_count = 0
    l_count = 0
    q_indices = []
    
    for i, c in enumerate(s):
        if c == 'W':
            w_count += 1
        elif c == 'L':
            l_count += 1
        elif c == '?':
            q_indices.append(i)
    
    current_diff = w_count - l_count
    
    # We want final |final_diff| = k
    # So we need final_diff = ±k
    
    # Let's try final_diff = k first
    target = k
    needed_change = target - current_diff
    
    # We need to distribute this change among the ? positions
    # Each ? can contribute +1 (if made W), -1 (if made L), or 0 (if made D)
    
    # We need sum(changes) = needed_change
    # with each change in {-1, 0, 1}
    
    # This is possible if |needed_change| <= number of ? and we can achieve it
    
    # We also need to ensure that during the sequence, we never have |diff| = k
    
    q_len = len(q_indices)
    
    # Try to find a valid assignment for target = k
    # We'll try to assign the changes greedily
    
    # First, if needed_change is 0, we can make all ? as D
    # Otherwise, we need to make some W and some L
    
    # Let's try to place W first, then L, to help us reach target
    # We need to distribute the change
    
    # We can make x positions as W (+1 each), y positions as L (-1 each), rest as D (0)
    # then x - y = needed_change
    # x + y <= q_len
    
    # For each possible y from 0 to q_len:
    #   x = needed_change + y
    #   if x >= 0 and x + y <= q_len:
    #       we can achieve this
    
    # For each such (x, y), try to build a valid sequence
    
    for y in range(q_len + 1):
        x = needed_change + y
        if x >= 0 and x + y <= q_len:
            # We have x W's and y L's to place in the q_indices
            # We need to assign them to positions in q_indices
            
            # Try to assign them in a way that doesn't make |diff| = k at any point
            # We'll try to assign them in a specific order
            # Assign W to positions with large index first or small index first?
            
            # Simple: try to assign W to first x positions, L to next y positions
            positions = q_indices
            if len(positions) < x + y:
                continue
                
            # Create result array
            result = list(s)
            w_assigned = 0
            l_assigned = 0
            
            for i, c in enumerate(s):
                if c == '?':
                    if w_assigned < x:
                        result[i] = 'W'
                        w_assigned += 1
                    elif l_assigned < y:
                        result[i] = 'L'
                        l_assigned += 1
                    else:
                        result[i] = 'D'
            
            # Check if this assignment is valid
            diff = 0
            valid = True
            for i in range(n):
                if result[i] == 'W':
                    diff += 1
                elif result[i] == 'L':
                    diff -= 1
                if abs(diff) == k and i < n - 1:  # before the last hand
                    valid = False
                    break
            
            if valid and abs(diff) == k:
                return ''.join(result)
    
    # Try target = -k
    target = -k
    needed_change = target - current_diff
    
    for y in range(q_len + 1):
        x = needed_change + y
        if x >= 0 and x + y <= q_len:
            # We have x W's and y L's to place
            positions = q_indices
            if len(positions) < x + y:
                continue
                
            result = list(s)
            w_assigned = 0
            l_assigned = 0
            
            for i, c in enumerate(s):
                if c == '?':
                    if w_assigned < x:
                        result[i] = 'W'
                        w_assigned += 1
                    elif l_assigned < y:
                        result[i] = 'L'
                        l_assigned += 1
                    else:
                        result[i] = 'D'
            
            diff = 0
            valid = True
            for i in range(n):
                if result[i] == 'W':
                    diff += 1
                elif result[i] == 'L':
                    diff -= 1
                if abs(diff) == k and i < n - 1:  # before the last hand
                    valid = False
                    break
            
            if valid and abs(diff) == k:
                return ''.join(result)
    
    return "NO"

print(solve())
```

Wait, I see a bug in my logic. The condition should be that we never have |diff| = k at any point except possibly at the very end. So in the simulation, we should only check for |diff| = k when i < n-1 (before the last hand).
